## 2026-07-01 (★★ RESUME HERE — LATEST ★★) — ⚠ CORRECTION: the 2026-06-30 "transient short-read" diagnosis was **WRONG**. Region 1's real, **reproducible** failure is a **symmetry-check failure** caused by ~11 **monomorphic (MAC=0-in-AFR) variants** → plink emits `NaN` LD → `NaN != NaN` breaks the symmetry check. **Systemic** across the 276 windows. Decision = **DROP MAC=0 variants** (Carter, 2026-07-01). **DO NOT FIRE** until the drop-monomorphic fix lands + a region-1 re-run passes `read_square_bin`.

**What actually happened (corrected):** on 120 GB, fire #3's region-1 `.ld.bin` convert reached `read_square_bin`, which **raised `square LD is not symmetric`** — proven by running `read_square_bin` directly on the intact 42 GB `.ld.bin` (deterministic RAISE, not transient). Pinpoint diagnostic: **12 NaN entries across 11 rows**, clustered in adjacent pairs/triples, diagonals still 1.0 → the fingerprint of monomorphic/zero-variance variants (plink `--r` gives `0/0 → NaN`). My earlier "transient" story anchored on a **stale fire-#1 TSV row** (`n_var mismatch / 0 rows`, chr-prefix-bug era, carried forward by `append_panel_row`'s dedup) and a determinism replay that tested the **wrong check** (the `n_var` count, which passes) — the symmetry check was never exercised until the AoU agent ran it. **The retry-on-zero guard (`27af416`) is harmless but does NOT fix this** — the convert hits the same NaNs regardless.

**FIX (in progress):** exclude MAC=0 variants from the LD computation (leaning `--mac 1` + `--write-snplist` at the plink step, threading the retained variant list into the `.npz`/`n_var` accounting — mechanism to be confirmed by the fix's research phase). Routed to `/gsd-quick --research --validate`. **VERIFICATION BEFORE FULL FIRE:** after the NCSU fix lands + pushes, the AoU agent re-runs **region 1 ONLY** and confirms `read_square_bin` passes (no NaN, symmetric) — only then launch the full 276. VM stays n1-standard-32 (NO respec — OOM concern remains resolved). Both OOM fixes (`3eac803`/`ff9e66f`) and the chr-prefix fix (`baeb925`) are still proven-good. **Authoritative resume = `.planning/HANDOFF.json` + this block.** **SUPERSEDES the 2026-06-30 block below.**

## 2026-06-30 (SUPERSEDED by 2026-07-01 above — the "transient" diagnosis was WRONG; real cause = monomorphic-NaN symmetry failure) — FIRE #3 (n1-standard-32 / 120 GB, `1b45d43`) PROVED BOTH OOM FIXES, then hit what I mis-diagnosed as a transient `.bim` short-read on region 1 → banked 0/276 (resume-safe). Retry-on-zero guard landed (`260630-rn4` @ `27af416`) — harmless but NOT the fix. **VM STAYS n1-standard-32 (NO respec)**

**The OOM fixes are exonerated; the 4th 0/276 cause was a transient, not a code bug.** The AoU UI offered no 128 GB option (only n1-standard-8..96, n2-standard-8/16), and n1-standard-64 (240 GB) busts the $500 cap — so Carter (AskUserQuestion) chose **n1-standard-32 (32 vCPU / 120 GB)**; the real RAM need is the ~40 GiB dense matrix, and 120 GB clears it to ~168k vars (region 1 = 102,421). Fire #3 (PID 432, HEAD `1b45d43`) reached region 1: plink emitted the 102,421-var `.ld.bin` (41,960,244,964 B), and the `.npz` **convert AND `content_verify_npz` both ran with NO `dmesg` OOM** — **both OOM fixes (`3eac803` + `ff9e66f`) are now proven on real data at scale**. But region 1 recorded `status=error: n_var mismatch ... the window .bim has 0 rows`.

**~6 read-only dig rounds PROVED no static defect.** Ruled out, in order: (1) stale/modified src — `md5 e99fd817…` == committed `1b45d43`; (2) chr-literal verify — the deployed `_window_bim_n_var` uses `_chrom_match_key` on both sides (line 284) and returns 102421 on the real `.bim`; (3) function regression `a5a5f9f`→`1b45d43` — byte-identical (fire #2 ran the SAME fn and PASSED this check, reaching the convert); (4) manifest float64 promotion — manifest `md5 62d1fb0…` == committed, `chr` dtype `int64`, no NaN, region1 chr = `int 1`; (5) interpreter/pandas divergence — loop `python` == repro `python3` == `/opt/conda/envs/jupyter`, pandas 2.3.2; (6) **determinism** — the EXACT driver path (`_read_manifest` → AFR `r1` → `_window_bim_n_var` → `_n_var_from_ld_bin` on the intact `.ld.bin`) **REPLAYS to `window_n_var=102421 == bin_n_var=102421` (MATCH)**. The float-chrom mechanism (`_chrom_match_key(1.0)='1.0'≠'1'`→0 rows) was *proven possible but ruled out* (runtime chr is int). Cohort `.bim` full+stable (883,834,306 B, mtime **Jun 27**, 20,767,864 lines). **Conclusion: a one-off TRANSIENT short read of `bim_path.read_text()` at the instant plink finished the 42 GB `.ld.bin` write.** Non-reproducible, no code bug. (Lesson reinforced: verify the runtime cause on real data before shipping — an early "verify does a chr-literal compare" AND a "`_read_manifest` reads float" hypothesis were both DISPROVEN on the VM before any code was touched, per `[[feedback_verify_assumption_before_shipping]]`.)

**The loop is already resume-safe per region** — an errored region has no `.npz` → it recomputes on re-fire; fire #3 shows "0/276" only because we STOPPED it (`kill 432`) at region 1 to investigate (left alone it would have banked ~275/276). Carter chose to add a **guard** so a transient self-heals IN-run.

**GUARD (quick `260630-rn4`, LANDED + GREEN):** reusable `_window_bim_n_var_retry_on_zero(...expect_nonzero...)` — when the square-path window read returns 0 but plink emitted a non-trivial `.ld.bin` (`bin_n_var>0`), re-read the `.bim` up to 3× (0.5 s sleep); a recovery logs a LOUD `WARN` and proceeds; a persistent 0 returns unchanged so the caller raises the **byte-identical** `ValueError` mismatch (`status=error`, loop continues — no semantics change). ## 2026-06-29 (SUPERSEDED by 2026-06-30 above — the 128 GB respec plan is OBSOLETE; fire #3 ran on 120 GB and PROVED both OOM fixes) — RE-FIRED LOOP OOM-KILLED ON REGION 1 (banked 0/276 AGAIN — different bug): the `.npz` convert step blew 64 GiB on a full-matrix symmetry check; FIXED + pushed (`3eac803`); VM STOPPED by Carter; re-fire on **n2-highmem-16 (128 GB)**

**The chr-prefix fix worked — a NEW bug surfaced one step later.** The 2026-06-28 re-fire (PID 6577, `a5a5f9f`) reached region 1, and plink's `--r square bin4` compute **finished and emitted the 102,421-variant `.ld.bin`** (the chr-prefix fix is **proven** — verify agreed, the window matched). But the very next step — the Python `.ld.bin`→`.npz` convert/bank — **OOM-killed** (`anon-rss ~61 GiB` on the 64 GB VM). Banked **0/276** again, for an unrelated reason. `df` was clean (42%) — this was a **RAM** ceiling, not the disk/chr-prefix issues.

**Root cause (confirmed):** in `src/python/plink_ld_to_npz.py`, `read_square_bin()` verified symmetry with `np.allclose(m, m.T, atol=1e-4)`. At n_var=102,421 the dense float32 matrix `m` is ~39 GiB; `np.allclose(m, m.T)` materializes **several full `n_var²` float32 temporaries** (`m-m.T`, `abs(...)`, `atol+rtol*abs(b)`, the bool mask), each ~39 GiB, **on top of** `m` → peak >64 GiB → kernel OOM-kill. **`np.savez_compressed` is NOT the killer** (numpy streams the array into the zip in chunks); the diagonal check is tiny. Same dense-collect OOM class the Hail path already guarded (`_max_safe_to_numpy_n_var`/`_dense_footprint_bytes`); the native-plink converter never got that guard. Third format/memory bug in this loop (after triangle-flag + chr-prefix) → per `[[feedback_extract_reusable_utilities]]` the fix is a reusable utility + failing-first regression.

**Fix landed + (to be) PUSHED — commit `3eac803`:** added module-level `_is_symmetric_blocked(m, atol, block=1024)` — compares `block` rows at a time against the matching transposed column block, bounding the transient to ~`block*n_var*4` bytes (~420 MB at n_var≈1e5) while enforcing the **identical** invariant. `read_square_bin` line ~171 now calls it; the `ValueError` message is **byte-identical**, the diagonal check + no-copy `reshape(...).astype(copy=False)` view are unchanged. Region-1 peak after the fix ≈ 45 GiB. **TDD (failing-first):** 3 new tests (`_is_symmetric_blocked` accept/reject; agreement with `np.allclose(m, m.T)` across block-boundary edge cases incl. `block>n`; `read_square_bin` still raises on asymmetric input). Targeted `tests/m3/test_plink_ld_to_npz.py` **17 passed**; full `tests/m3` **302 passed / 30 skipped** (above the 287+ baseline; R/reticulate loader tests RAN via the `m3-r-ld` Rscript pin; 30 skips are pre-existing Hail/synthetic-MT/chain gates). Quick task `260629-2im`.

**★ BLAST-RADIUS CAUGHT A SECOND OOM HEAD (fixed @ `ff9e66f`, quick `260629-402`) — the `260629-2im` fix alone would have OOM'd the re-fire AGAIN.** A 4-investigator read-only blast-radius sweep of `3eac803` confirmed the predicate is provably equivalent (927 cases, 0 disagreements) AND the `.npz`/callers/resume are all safe — but two investigators independently found that `content_verify_npz` (the in-process per-region D-M3-10 gate, `src/python/run_native_ld_panel.py:228`, run right after the convert at `:475`) RELOADS the full `.npz` (`np.load` → ~39 GiB) and re-runs the **same unbounded** `np.allclose(ld, ld.T, atol=1e-4)` → **~4× matrix ≈ 156 GiB at n_var=102,421 > the 128 GB VM** → region 1 would OOM-kill again, one function later (0/276). Fix @ `ff9e66f`: square branch → reuse `pln._is_symmetric_blocked`; banded branch (`:234`, same class via a full `np.triu` copy — off the square path but in the same shared gate) → new co-located `pln._strict_upper_is_zero_blocked` (block-wise `np.triu(block, k=i+1)`). Reason strings byte-identical; diagonal + flag checks untouched. TDD failing-first; targeted **48 passed**; full `tests/m3` **305 passed / 30 skipped** (≥302 baseline). **Both OOM heads on the square convert→verify path are now bounded** (peak ≈ matrix + ~0.5 GiB ≈ 40 GiB for region 1). **VM STOPPED by Carter** (idle-billing halted; stop ≠ delete — VM + reattachable PD + 379 GB local bfile preserved, resume-safe). **Carter DECIDED: re-fire on `n2-highmem-16` (128 GB)** — the lean fix makes region 1 fit on 64 GB (~45 GiB peak), but the bare dense array scales `n_var²`, so an unknown region >~115k variants could still crowd a 64 GB box deep into the ~11-day run; 128 GB gives headroom up to ~175k vars. ~$60-67 total (was ~$45-50), under the $500 cap.

**The first fire banked 0/276 — fixed, re-fired clean.** The 2026-06-27 loop (PID 4237) ran ~17h and banked **nothing**: region 1 failed verify `n_var mismatch: .ld.bin implies 102421 but the window .bim has 0 rows`. **Root cause (confirmed on real AoU data):** the AoU GRCh38 cohort `.bim` uses **`chr`-prefixed** contigs (`chr1`) while `config/ld_regions.tsv` `chr` is **bare numeric** (`1`). plink1.9 normalizes the prefix (so `--chr 1` correctly built the 102,421-var `.ld.bin`), but `_window_bim_n_var` did a **literal** `str(parts[0]) == chrom` compare that never strips `chr` → 0 in-window rows → mismatch on every region. **Fix landed + PUSHED (origin == local == `a5a5f9f`):** `_chrom_match_key()` (strip leading `chr`/`CHR`, mirroring plink1.9) applied to **both** sides of the compare in `src/python/run_native_ld_panel.py`; kept-line content stays **verbatim** (downstream `ld_npz_to_rds.R` strips the prefix before the bare-`chr:pos` GWAS join, so no second mis-keying bug). Failing-first parametrized regression over all 4 prefix combos; `tests/m3` **28 + 14 passed**. Commits `baeb925` (fix+test) / `a5a5f9f` (docs). Quick task `260628-244`. Also repaired a pre-existing **git index dangling-blob corruption** (`.planning/WAVE-1-CLOSEOUT-CHECKLIST.md`, object missing from the store) via `git hash-object -w` to unblock the docs commit.

It could be at the gates, exporting, loop-firing, or blocked.

**Compute shape DECIDED this session = single-node n2-standard-16 Dataproc, serial (`--num-shards 1`), ~11 days, ~$250–300 on-demand.** Converted in place from the 25-node HAIL cluster `20604` (was Running **$24.07/hr** — reuse-as-is = ~$6.3k, killed). Single-node Dataproc masters can't be Spot, so ~$250–300 on-demand (not the dead 8-VM ~$50 dream). **`timeout 312h` wall doubles as a hard ~$293 cost ceiling.**

**The agent's sequence (autonomous):** stop 20604 → **convert to single node n2-standard-16 / ≥500 GB disk / HAIL retained** (DESTRUCTIVE disk-wipe — forensic mirror done first) → start (cheap) → **GATE A** running $/hr ~$0.9–1.0 + **GATE B** non-interactive self-delete permission dry-test (`gcloud dataproc clusters delete … --help`) → report → `git pull` to `10aaa6f` → **STEP 2 export-once** (`export_cohort_to_plink(AFR MT → LOCAL /home/jupyter/afr_cohort)`; verify `.bed` fits the disk) → fire the wrapped loop → 2–3 day liveness check-ins.

**⚠ KEY PATH CHANGE vs the prior handoff:** `--bfile-prefix` is now **LOCAL** (`/home/jupyter/afr_cohort`), **NOT** `gs://` — plink1.9 cannot read a `gs://` bfile. Only `--out-dir`/`--panel-tsv` are `gs://` (durable). The loop command:
```
nohup bash -c 'trap "gcloud dataproc clusters delete <CLUSTER> --region <REGION> --quiet" EXIT; \
  timeout 312h python src/python/run_native_ld_panel.py \
    --manifest config/ld_regions.tsv --bfile-prefix /home/jupyter/afr_cohort \
    --out-dir gs://rw-migration-aou-rw-476cdac2/ld/afr_native_panel/ \
    --scratch-dir /home/jupyter/native_ld_scratch --mode square --ancestry AFR --num-shards 1 \
    --panel-tsv gs://rw-migration-aou-rw-476cdac2/ld/afr_native_panel/m3-W2-native-plink-panel.tsv' \
  > ~/native_ld_loop.log 2>&1 &
```

TDD RED-first regression; **tests/m3 244 passed / 30 skipped / 0 failed** (`smoke_dev` py3.11+pandas — the `m3-r-ld` env has NO pandas). Debug session resolved → `.planning/debug/resolved/m3-W2-a2-dense-narrow-driver-oom-routing.md` + knowledge-base append. 0.40 fraction is conservative-correct (the float32 astype copy co-resides → true transient peak `n²×12`).

## 2026-06-18 — dev-10 LD fire KILLED: intractable on the dev cluster; Wave-2 RE-SCOPE needed (the finding that triggered the re-scope above)

**The dev-10 LD fire is DONE — killed, 0 regions completed. It proved a CAPACITY wall, not a correctness bug.** Cluster STOPPED, billing halted, orphan scratch deleted.

### What happened

- dev-10 fired on `20260617` (16× n2-standard-4 = 64 vCPU / 16 GB workers). **`region_00006`** (the *cheapest* A.3 region: n_var=122,678, AFR 73,122 samples) crawled its 900-block Stage-4 at **~0.9–2.1 blocks/min** (vs **~66** for the synthetic repro on the same cluster), and the **64 GB master CRASHED** during its **~65 GiB dense-scratch** phase. `region_00040` (xlarge, ×2) would be **~weeks/cell**. Killed; **0 regions completed**, no run log.
- **Root cause:** the repro used `--n-samples 2000`; the real cohorts are **73,122 / 220,098** samples, and the correlation matmul's inner dim **is** the sample count → **~36× (AFR) / ~110× (EUR)** more compute per block + 16 GB workers spilling + master can't sustain the dense scratch. The repro validated the **fix's correctness** (no driver-collect *hang*) but its 928 s wall-time **never represented real-cohort COST** — and we sized the fire off it.

### Settled (do NOT relitigate)

- **A.3 fix is CORRECT** (materialize-then-band; blocks advanced, no hang) — confirmed on the repro and region_00006. The problem is **capacity**, not the fix.
- **Ordering A vs B is settled** (keep A) — banded==dense under the radius scheme; **splitting**, not re-ordering, shrinks the dense intermediate.
- **Cohorts intact**, read-gate + bucket wiring proven (AFR 73,122 / EUR 220,098 read live).

### NEXT (RESUME HERE) — Wave-2 re-scope, NOT a re-fire

**2026-06-18 (resume) — DECISION: plan the FULL re-scope before any re-fire.** Carter confirmed the bigger-cluster path, and we agreed that a resize alone is insufficient because dev-10 itself contains `region_00040` (xlarge) — so the xlarge-region split (change #2) must land before the re-fire. Proceeding to `/gsd-plan-phase`.

Run **`/gsd-plan-phase`** for the re-scope per **`.planning/phases/m3-aou-afr-ld-panel-build/WAVE-2-RESCOPE-real-cohort-compute.md`** — three coupled changes:

1. **Cluster sized for real cohorts** — n2-highmem-16 workers (RAM stops the spill), 256–384 vCPU (push to quota), highmem master (the dense scratch crashed a 64 GB master).
2. **Split the ~16 xlarge regions** into ≤~75–100k-var sub-regions (drops cross-sub-region LD beyond the 50 Mb radius — already the accepted banded treatment; shrinks compute + scratch + driver load at once; the **load-bearing** change — resize alone does NOT make xlarge tractable). Touches `build_ld_region_manifest.py` / `compute_region_ld` / `ld_npz_to_rds.R`.
3. **Redo the ~1,117 cluster-h cost model** from real per-region rates (EUR ~3× AFR/block).

Teardown: orphan `…/ld/AFR_aou/bm/m2_region_00006.corr_scratch.bm` (~65 GiB) DELETED; cluster `20260617` STOPPED (synced repo + hail preserved if boot disk persists, else re-sync via GitHub ZIP). The 3 prep landmines (symlink `~/coloc_analysis`, pin `WORKSPACE_BUCKET`, `os.chdir` before loop) still apply to any re-fire. Primary structured resume = `.planning/HANDOFF.json`.

GATE-1.5 ✅ · GATE-0 ✅ · GATE-1 ✅ (cost MODEL needs redo) · **GATE-2 🔴 BLOCKED on the re-scope** · GATE-3 🔴 BLOCKED on the same.

## 2026-06-18 — A.3 ordering experiment DONE on cluster; fix VALIDATED; KEEP ordering A; B RETIRED; CR-01 reframed (SUPERSEDED by the dev-10 fire block above)

**The A.3 BlockMatrix-write hang is FIXED + CLUSTER-VALIDATED. The ordering experiment ran and the decision is made: KEEP ordering A, retire ordering B.** Quick `260618-3at`.

### What ran

- Cluster `20260617` (the 2-worker repro cluster) was **resized 2 → 16 workers** (n2-standard-4; the machine type is locked on resize, so we scaled count). `MASTER=yarn`, 64 concurrent tasks. The resize succeeding **diagnosed the original 24-worker prod-cluster failure as quota/size-bound, NOT project-level** (perimeter/credit).
- `scripts/a3_blockmatrix_lowering_repro.py --skip-old --n-var 130000 --n-samples 2000 --radius-bp 1000000 --budget-sec 3600`:
  - **Ordering A COMPLETED — 928.0 s**, valid `A/repro_A.bm`. The 21 s gap between the dense checkpoint and the final write = the mechanism working (final write reads concrete blocks). **First cluster validation of the deployed A.3 fix** (dev-10 paused before ever confirming it).
  - **Ordering B COMPLETED — 863.5 s**, valid `B/repro_B.bm`. B's `sparsify→checkpoint` (same IR shape as the OLD hung write) **did NOT reproduce the hang** — IR-shape caveat cleared.
  - OLD skipped (hang already proven by dev-10 + the prior 2-worker 0.21 MiB/s driver-bound grind).
- **DECISIVE (`--report-scratch-size`, full manifest):** `banded(B) == dense(A)` for **ALL 23 A.3 regions** — the manifest's `radius = span+500kb` (cap 50 Mb) is always ≥ span/2, so the band covers ~the whole region everywhere → **B saves scratch NOWHERE**.

### Decision

- **KEEP ordering A** (deployed + now cluster-validated). **Ordering B RETIRED** — byte-identical numerics, zero scratch benefit, nonzero change-risk. The `DRAFT-orderingB-band-before-checkpoint.md` patch is parked (revisit only if a future manifest adopts radius ≪ span).
- **CR-01 is ORDERING-INDEPENDENT:** region_00145 (~710k var) needs ~1.8 TiB of transient GCS scratch under A *or* B. The debug session `m3-W2-a3-blockmatrix-write-ir-lowering-hang` is **RESOLVED** (the hang bug is fixed + cluster-confirmed).
- Cluster `20260617` being **Stopped** (not deleted — preserves the verified script + `hail==0.2.135` install).

### NEXT (RESUME HERE)

- **GATE-2 dev-10 can resume on ordering A** (the fix is validated; ~60 GB scratch for one dev region is tolerable). Re-fire the previously-hanging A.3 cell (`m2_region_00006`), verify the `.bm` at the **data layer** (`_assert_blockmatrix_written` shape + `gsutil du` of `.bm/parts`, NOT `_SUCCESS`) → resume the rest of dev-10 → AOU-4 validation memo.

The pre-write `mt.repartition(2048)` (`shuffle=True`) after `split_multi_hts` built a Spark RangePartitioner by **sampling row keys across all partitions** → a driver-side `SpillingCollectIterator` (jstack: `TableValue.mapRows`) funneled ~2,077 partitions to the master, spilling to `/tmp` (~38 min, ~1.4s driver CPU, 0 executor threads). Doubly redundant (naive_coalesce already set count=2048; the checkpoint write+reread already yields a clean partitioner). **Fix** (Hail-team "repartition after write, not before"): drop the pre-write repartition; rebalance on the post_split checkpoint **read-back** via `hl.read_matrix_table(ckpt, _n_partitions=_post_split_read_partitions(...))` — on-disk partition index, no key sampling, no driver gather. New pure helper `_post_split_read_partitions` + `_COHORT_TARGET_PARTITIONS=2048`. Commits **`e23c081`** (code+tests) + **`0f92428`** (debug doc `.planning/debug/m3-gateb-load-qc-cohort-driver-collect.md`). 3 RED-first regression guards; tests/m3 **112 passed / 27 skipped**; Track-4 guard `_assert_checkpoint_nonempty` byte-identical (verified via diff scope). **This bug would also have crippled the full genome-wide fire.**

## 2026-06-01 session — CHECK C RESOLVED CODE-SIDE (env-derive AUX base)

(Resumed via `/gsd-resume-work`; q04 docs pushed `a83f1c2..48da268`. Then Carter: "Solve / resolve this problem so we can move forward" re: the stuck CHECK C/D.)

**CHECK C is no longer a blocker — resolved in code, not deferred to an env-capture.** Quick task `260601-cca`; commits **`f4c495c`** (feat + tests) + **`e196ac1`** (AOU-0 precheck reframe) + this state commit. The root reason CHECK C was a gate: the driver pinned the AoU auxiliary tables to a hardcoded `CDR_VERSION = "v8"` literal, so every CDR advance needed a manual Workbench path-verification AND a code edit (it bit us at v7→v8). 

**Fix:** `_resolve_aux_base(mt_path)` in `src/python/aou_ld_panel.py` env-derives the AUX base from the WGS ACAF MT path the cohort is built from — split on the stable `/wgs/short_read/snpindel/` infix (aux/ is a documented sibling of acaf_threshold/, verified 2026-05-01 Run 2), rebuild `<prefix>/wgs/short_read/snpindel/aux`. Resolution order: explicit arg → `$WGS_ACAF_THRESHOLD_MULTI_HAIL_PATH` → hardcoded literal fallback (offline/local/tests). `load_qc_cohort` resolves anc/rel paths from the derived base (override seam preserved); `_collect_provenance` records the RESOLVED paths (truthful sidecar). Dead `RELATEDNESS_FULL_PATH` removed; docstring corrected. **Effect: a CDR advance (v8/v9/...) is a no-op for AUX-path resolution.** CHECK C downgrades from a gate to a compute-free confirmation now in AOU-0 precheck Cell 2 (prints resolved base + `gsutil`-confirms the 2 tables list before any spend). Applied UNCONDITIONALLY per [[feedback_rigor_over_speed]] (strictly more correct than the hardcoded literal even if R9 still uses `/v8/`).

**Method (workflow-orchestrated, ultracode):** understand workflow (3 Explore agents → blast radius + path-structure evidence + test conventions) → TDD (12 pure-Python regression tests RED→GREEN, incl. the v9 CHECK-C guard + a CDR-drift invalidation contract test; `tests/m3` 86 passed / 27 skipped / 0 failed) → adversarial-review workflow (3 refutation lenses). **2 "blocking" findings REJECTED with rationale** (receiving-code-review discipline): (1) "silent fallback to v8 literal on missing v9 path" would pair v9 genotypes with v8 ancestry calls = the DEC-2026-05-01-01 hazard → ancestry keeps its correct hard-fail; (2) "exclude source_mt_path so checkpoints reuse across CDR" = data-integrity bug → the R8→R9 invalidation is PRE-EXISTING via source_mt_path (line 341) and CORRECT; the env-derive change adds aux fields collinear with it → zero new invalidation. Full disposition in `.planning/quick/260601-cca-env-derive-aux-base-close-check-c/SUMMARY.md`.

**Status:** CHECK D ✅ (q04, Hail 0.2.135). CHECK C ✅ code-side. **Next:** push to origin (fix is inert on AoU until pushed — clone pulls from origin) → AOU-0 precheck (compute-free) in a CDR-wired Standard Analysis env → chr22 smoke (Carter holds trigger). Track 4 patches remain load-bearing (mechanism live). 1000G AFR remains smoke-fail safety net only ([[feedback_no_1000g_ld_pivot]]).

| 260501-v9q | **CR-001 regression pytest + tests/m3 scaffold parity — locks the post-CR-001 resolver behavior (independent `region_id` and `region_safe` placeholders) behind a fail-fast regression-bite test.** Closes the regression-prevention loop opened by the m3-aou-afr-ld-panel-build code-review fix pass (commit `6d2e753`, CR-001 finding). **Pre-CR-001 root cause:** `resolve_ld_path()` substituted both `{region_id}` and `{region_safe}` placeholders with the same `region_id` value; the AoU chain head template uses `{region_id}` (e.g., `m2_region_00067.rds`) while 1kg/HGDP/UKBB tails use `{region_safe}` (e.g., `FTO_16q12.rds`), so the bug silently caused the 1kg fallback to never resolve when only `region_safe`-named files existed on disk. **What this task adds:** 3 regression tests appended to `tests/m3/test_ld_panel_resolver.py` (8 → 11 tests; existing 8 byte-identical except one new `import re`): (1) `test_resolver_distinct_region_id_and_region_safe_aou_head` — positive AoU-head contract with DISTINCT `region_id="m2_region_00067"` + `region_safe="FTO_16q12"`, asserts AoU-bucket `.rds` resolves; (2) `test_resolver_distinct_region_id_and_region_safe_1kg_fallback` — **the decisive regression-bite test**: AoU bucket EMPTY + 1kg has only `FTO_16q12.rds`; asserts resolver returns the 1kg path. Confirmed via temporary pre-fix simulation in `src/python/ld_panel.py` line 85 (single-arg substitution restored): test B FAILS with `FileNotFoundError: No LD panel found for m2_region_00067 AFR`; fix restored, all 11 pass. (3) `test_finemap_smk_calls_resolver_with_both_kwargs` — static-grep guard against future call-site regressions: regex-asserts that `src/snakemake/rules/finemap.smk` calls `resolve_ld_path` with both `region_id=` and `region_safe=` kwargs. Without this static check, a refactor that drops one kwarg would re-introduce the silent fallback bug because the resolver's back-compat default (`region_safe=None` → `region_safe=region_id`) would mask it. **Nuance recorded in SUMMARY:** Test A (AoU-head) does NOT bite pre-fix code in isolation because the AoU template uses ONLY `{region_id}` — substituting both placeholders with `region_id` still resolves the AoU file correctly. Test B is the only regression-bite test; Test A is retained as a positive AoU-contract guard, Test C as static call-site coverage. **Hermetic:** `tmp_path` + `Path.touch()`; no Hail, no AoU, no network; reuses existing `_build_config` and `_touch` helpers (no duplication). **Scaffold parity:** added empty `tests/m3/__init__.py` aligning the m3 test directory with the precedent set by `tests/m1/__init__.py` (m2 still lacks one — out of scope for this task). **1 atomic commit, scoped via explicit `git add <path>`:** `66d6b8f` `test(quick-260501-v9q): add CR-001 regression tests for ld_panel resolver`. **Verification:** `pytest tests/m3/test_ld_panel_resolver.py -v` → **11 passed in 0.50s** (Python 3.11.15 / `/rs1/researchers/c/ckclinto/conda_envs/smoke_dev/bin/python`). **Project rules honored:** explicit-path staging only (no `git add .` / `-A`); no conda activation guidance to user; HPC PATH preface used in verification. Pre-existing dirty files (`.claude/settings.json`, `.planning/config.json`, parallel-workstream `results_lsweep_*` dirs from ta-sh2b3 SuSiE max-iter sweep, `wave4_dispatch_tracker_v4_addendum_supervisor_orphan.json`, `260429-utt/bjobs.tsv`) NOT staged. **Out-of-scope (not addressed by this task):** other CR-001 sibling concerns (CR-002 Hail row-order verification, CR-003/CR-004 follow-ups), the 3 skipped-from-fix-pass items (WR-005/WR-006/WR-009 — see REVIEW-FIX.md for rationales), tests/m2 scaffold parity (m2 also lacks `__init__.py` but is unrelated to CR-001). | 260512-ldj | **NCSU AOU-1 reference template synced to AoU-working init pattern — audit-driven re-analysis closing the stale-template producer/consumer drift surfaced 2026-05-12.** Follow-on to quick 260512-jd9 (helper extraction + checkpoint-URI patch). The NCSU canonical reference notebook `.planning/notebooks/AOU-1_template.ipynb` Cell 1 carried the old `init_hail()` invocation that's empirically broken on AoU's Dataproc + YARN cluster: `hl.init(spark_conf={"spark.executor.cores": "1"})` is silently overridden by the cluster's spark-defaults.conf + YARN `yarn.scheduler.minimum-allocation-vcores` floor, yielding `spark.executor.cores=2` in the live SparkConf despite the dict-supplied conf — defeating the cores=1 belt-and-suspenders pair of DEC-2026-05-04-01's `mt.naive_coalesce(2048)` v8 partition-explosion OOM remediation (commit `8cc6f64`). Carter confirmed empirically 2026-05-12 during the Cell 2 init fire that the working lever is `PYSPARK_SUBMIT_ARGS="--conf spark.executor.cores=1 pyspark-shell"` set BEFORE any pyspark/hail import — this applies at the spark-submit boundary, which beats spark-defaults.conf in Spark's config-precedence order. Canonical pattern baked into auto-memory `[[aou-dataproc-pyspark-submit-args]]` for cross-session persistence. **Edit strategy:** Python helper script (NOT hand-edit JSON) — load `.ipynb` via `json.load`, pre-state assert (8 cells, cell[1] contains `init_hail()`), split cell[1] into two new cells (Cell 1a PYSPARK_SUBMIT_ARGS injection + Cell 1b direct hl.init with spark_conf dict + verification asserts on cores=1 and `_qc_checkpoint_uri` import from commit `36e8062`), append sync-note paragraph to cell[0] markdown title, post-state assert (9 cells), `json.dump(indent=1)` to preserve nbformat conventions. **Cell-count delta:** 8 → 9 (cell[1] split into cell[1a]+cell[1b], net +1; cells 2-7 byte-identical; cell[0] markdown appended). **Verification (all 10 PASS):** json.load asserts 9 cells / `PYSPARK_SUBMIT_ARGS` grep = 7 / `spark.executor.cores` grep = 5 / `_qc_checkpoint_uri` grep = 6 / `init_hail()` grep = 0 (old wrapper call removed) / `feedback_aou_dataproc_pyspark_submit_args` grep = 2 (markdown sync-note + Cell 1b docstring cross-ref) / `hl.init(` grep = 3 / `load_qc_cohort` grep = 6 / `len(overlap) == 0` grep = 1 / JSON validity round-trip exit 0. One in-spec anomaly: `naive_coalesce(2048)` grep = 2 (cited in Cell 1a + Cell 1b comments as pairing cross-references per canonical-pattern documentation; reviewer-friendly + reviewer-defensible). **1 atomic commit `5389a88`** with two files (.ipynb + STATE.md), pushed to `origin/main` (`d7a221a..5389a88 main -> main`). Subject token `(m3-W1-template-pyspark-submit-args-sync)`; body uses "audit-driven re-analysis" / "stale-template sync" framing per [[feedback_original_research_framing]]; zero forbidden tokens (cleanup/revision/salvage). **Why this matters (downstream consumers of the NCSU template):** future re-fires of M3 Wave 1 (e.g., AoU CDR v9 trigger; AoU workspace re-creation; reviewer replication audit; second author onboarding to the repo) will consume `.planning/notebooks/AOU-1_template.ipynb` as the canonical recipe. Pre-sync, anyone who pasted Cell 1 verbatim into a fresh AoU Workbench notebook would hit the executor RegionPool OOM during the Cell 3 fire. Post-sync, the paste-ready template self-verifies the cores=1 lever before any compute spend. **Carter next-action:** None — AoU-side live notebook already running with the working pattern this session; this sync is reproducibility / future re-fires / reviewer audit trail for the canonical reference template. **Cross-refs:** predecessor `260512-jd9` (`36e8062` `_qc_checkpoint_uri` helper extraction + 4 regression tests), `8cc6f64` (m3-W2 `naive_coalesce(2048)` RegionPool OOM remediation), DEC-2026-05-04-01 (originating decision binding the patch family), [[aou-dataproc-pyspark-submit-args]] (canonical pattern memory baked this session). | 260528-jvd | **m3-W1 Track 4 defensive-code patches — contents-validated MT write protocol lands as 7 atomic commits with TDD discipline; closes the silent-empty-MT failure class that produced the 2026-05-21 ~$2,100 catastrophe.** Patches the resume-gate hole + write-side validation gap exposed by the m3-W1 empty-MT catastrophe: Hail's `mt.checkpoint()` writes `_SUCCESS` on driver-side tasks-reported-complete accounting WITHOUT validating output contents — under `spark.executor.cores=1/mem=5g` (necessary for v8 partition-explosion OOM remediation), executor tasks can silently truncate after writing 35-byte Parquet column-metadata footer stubs, leaving an MT directory with `_SUCCESS` + rows-stubs + absent `entries/entries/parts/` + `count_rows()=0 + count_cols()=0` on read-back. The refactored `load_qc_cohort` at HEAD did NOT defend against this — `_has_checkpoint()` only checks `_SUCCESS`, so a stub MT triggered `RESUME_FROM_POST_SAMPLE_QC` and Phase 3 fired `variant_qc` on an empty MT, producing another empty `_SUCCESS`-marked MT. **7 atomic commits with TDD RED→GREEN sequence:** (1) `59e914b` test(m3-W1, RED) — 3 regression tests for `_validate_checkpoint_populated` (`rejects_stub_entries` / `rejects_empty_entries_dir` / `has_checkpoint_vs_validate_diverge_on_stub_mt`); shared `_make_stub_mt` helper builds the exact catastrophe pattern (`_SUCCESS` + `metadata.json.gz` + 35-byte Parquet-footer rows-stub + optionally-empty `entries/entries/parts/`); RED state confirmed via ImportError on the not-yet-existent helper. (2) `86c8a56` feat(m3-W1, GREEN) — `_validate_checkpoint_populated(uri, *, min_entries_bytes=MIN_ENTRIES_FILE_BYTES=1024)` helper in `src/python/aou_ld_panel.py`; validates `_SUCCESS` + `entries/entries/parts/` exists + at least one entries file > 1 KB threshold (cleanly discriminates footer stubs from real partition contents); `file://` scheme uses pathlib, `gs://` defers to `hl.hadoop_ls`; defensive try/except returns False on any FS error; also amends `_has_checkpoint` docstring with WARNING clause directing new callers to the validated helper. (3) `4c75ca2` fix(m3-W1) — swap `_has_checkpoint(ckpt_post_sqc)` and `_has_checkpoint(ckpt_post_split)` calls in `load_qc_cohort`'s auto-resume state machine for `_validate_checkpoint_populated()`; adds explicit catastrophe-pattern elif branches that match "`_has_checkpoint` True AND `_validate_checkpoint_populated` False" (the W1 signature) and force `auto_fresh = True` with a loud WARN print so the stub-MT pathology surfaces in the cell's stdout. (4) `88ab0c5` feat(m3-W1) — `_assert_checkpoint_nonempty(mt, uri, *, phase)` helper + invokes after each of 3 `mt.checkpoint()` sites (intermediate 1 / intermediate 2 / final); raises `RuntimeError` on `count_rows()==0 OR count_cols()==0` with pointer to catastrophe debug doc; ~10-30 sec per checkpoint × 3 = ~1-2 min total overhead per cohort (trivial against the 60+ h monolithic-run baseline; per `[[feedback_rigor_over_speed]]`). (5) `9b4317f` feat(m3-W1) — inserts 3 new code cells in `.planning/notebooks/AOU-1_template.ipynb`: Cell 3.5 (validates `mt_afr_qc.mt`), Cell 4.5 (validates `mt_afr_pca_selfid_qc.mt`), Cell 5.5 (validates `mt_eur_qc.mt`); each fires `gsutil du -s` against the corresponding `entries/entries/parts/` subpath and asserts size > 1 GB floor; halts the notebook BEFORE downstream cells define cohorts on top of an empty MT — the exact misordering that compounded the W1 catastrophe over 65h. (6) `4b321c2` docs(m3-W1) — `.planning/WAVE-1-CLOSEOUT-CHECKLIST.md` STEP 3 upgraded from `_SUCCESS`+`metadata.json.gz` listing to contents-validating verification with explicit "CRITICAL — m3-W1 catastrophe lesson" callout at top, `entries/: OK (X.YZ GB)` pass criterion, HONEST_FINDING disposition path on catastrophe-pattern fail, and a belt-and-suspenders Hail-side read-probe from a fresh Python subprocess (NOT the original kernel — JVM-cached IR masked the W1 catastrophe). (7) `bfe5f0e` decision(m3-W1) — `D-M3-10: MT write verification protocol — contents-validated, not _SUCCESS-only` decision block in `.planning/phases/m3-aou-afr-ld-panel-build/m3-CONTEXT.md` `<decisions>` + assumption #10 echo in `<assumptions>` per the D-M3-03/D-M3-09 echo pattern; locks the protocol as a phase-level requirement spanning Waves 1+2+4+5 (no new code-side `_has_checkpoint(ckpt_*)` callers may be introduced for resume-gate semantics). **Test sweep:** 38 PASSED + 11 SKIPPED + 0 FAILED on `tests/m3/test_aou_ld_panel_local.py` via `/rs1/researchers/c/ckclinto/conda_envs/smoke_dev/bin/pytest` at every commit boundary (no regression at any patch); 11 SKIPs are the existing live-Hail tests (`@_require_hail()`) that gracefully skip on smoke_dev per [[project_python_311_pin]] and will run on the next AoU env with Hail installed. **Verification gates (7/7 PASS):** `_validate_checkpoint_populated` defined in `aou_ld_panel.py` / both resume-gate sites use validated helper / 4 `_assert_checkpoint_nonempty` occurrences (1 def + 3 callers) / 3 new regression test names present / 3 new AOU-1 cells (3.5/4.5/5.5) present per nbformat round-trip / 8 `entries/entries/parts` + catastrophe references in closeout checklist / D-M3-10 grep returns 2 (decision header + assumption echo). **Behavioral net change (resume-gate truth table):** populated MT + valid sidecar → RESUME (preserved); `_SUCCESS`-absent MT → FRESH (preserved); orphan MT (sidecar absent) → WARN + auto_fresh (preserved); sidecar mismatch → RuntimeError (preserved); ****stub MT (`_SUCCESS` + empty entries) → WARN + auto_fresh (W1 catastrophe DEFENDED — pre-patch this triggered RESUME into empty MT)****. Cost: $0 (all NCSU-side; no AoU compute). Carter-state: m3-W1 Track 4 lane CLEAR; Tracks 1 (AoU credit recovery; Abby Doyle engaged, awaiting engineering response) + 2 (1000G AFR safety-net pivot decision; deferred pending Abby's team response) + 3 (NCSU v7→v8 forensic investigation; COMPLETE) continue in parallel; patches apply REGARDLESS of which LD substrate Wave 2 ends up using. **Cross-refs:** root-cause analysis at `.planning/debug/m3-W1-empty-mt-catastrophe.md` (40 KB); patch source `.planning/quick/260521-w1-catastrophe-handoff/HANDOFF.md` §Track 4; precedent refactor `.planning/quick/260518-qcr-load-qc-cohort-resilience-refactor/260518-qcr-SUMMARY.md` (introduced the unsafe `_has_checkpoint()` resume gate that this task fixes); memories `[[feedback_aou_success_marker_not_evidence_of_data]]` + `[[feedback_hail_checkpoint_contract_violation]]` + amended `[[feedback_aou_spark_ui_stack_trace_verification]]` + `[[feedback_failed_to_honest_finding]]` (HONEST_FINDING disposition rule) + `[[feedback_rigor_over_speed]]` (cost/benefit framing). ### Session 2026-05-20 — Wave-1 monolithic completion + iframe-hostile stack-trace verification + workbench env pause

**Background.** Resumed from 2026-05-18 session: AoU Cell 3-7 fired via Run All Below on un-refactored monolithic code at 2026-05-18 03:14:12 UTC against 16× n1-highmem-16 (256 vCPU) Dataproc cluster. Refactor pushed to origin/main as `50f071c` on 2026-05-18 evening.

**Key narrative:**

1. **Multi-day Cell 3-7 monolithic execution** (2026-05-18 03:14 UTC → 2026-05-20 19:08 UTC = ~67h). Three wide compute+write stages observed, each followed by canonical 82-task finalize-cascade (rows component write + metadata.json.gz + _SUCCESS markers):
   - **Stage 36** (7.7h, 4090 tasks) — Cell 3 MT #1 `mt_afr_qc.mt` write (already verified 2026-05-19 via gsutil scrollback)
   - **Stage 45** (9.5h, 4090 tasks) — Cell 4 MT #2 `mt_afr_pca_selfid_qc.mt` write
   - **Stage 62** (16.5h, 4090 tasks) — Cell 5 MT #3 `mt_eur_qc.mt` write
   
   Cell 6 (disjoint-cohort check via `mt.s.collect()` + set arithmetic) likely fired in the ~18s gap between Stage 70 (last MT #3 cascade stage) and Stage 71 (Cell 7's first count_rows).

2. **Cell 7 count_rows velocity collapse** — Stage 71 (Cell 7's `mt_afr_selfid.count_rows()` for the cohort_summary_m3.tsv `n_variants` column) started 2026-05-20 19:08:06 UTC. 4090 tasks at SparkBackend.scala:450. Velocity progression measured across multiple polls:
   - 27 min: 0/4090 (JIT warmup phase, expected)
   - 43 min: 0/4090 (warmup persisting)
   - 2.7h: 252/4090 (window velocity 2.12 t/min — slow but progressing)
   - 3.5h: 265/4090 (window velocity collapsed to 0.27 t/min — 8× deceleration)
   
   Projection at terminal velocity: Stage 71 alone needs ~9-43h more; Stage 72 (mt_eur.count_rows) similar; total $800-$9,000 additional burn. The slow per-task time is structurally inconsistent with Hail's textbook count_rows-on-read-back-MT (which should use cached partition metadata).

3. **Pivot to mid-Cell-7 pause** — Path B1 was selected on 2026-05-19 ~14:30 UTC (cost-equivalent with B2 kill+refactor at MT #2/#3 inflection; iframe-broken; healthy run track record at the time). The Stage 71 velocity collapse changed the math: pause-and-defer-TSV vs continue-grind saved $800-$9,000. The TSV is a 3-row validation-memo summary that Wave 2/3/4 don't require (Cell 8 markdown: "after Wave 2 dev fire signoff, Wave 5 close-out task"). Sample counts derivable from print stdout / refactored re-fire; variant counts derivable from MT rows-component metadata at any future time.

4. **Stack-trace verification (the decisive analysis)** — Carter's correct caution that "if Stage 62 was actually MT #3's write being in-progress (not done), pausing forfeits MT #3" forced rigorous verification under iframe-+terminal-hostility. Methodology:
   - Click `+details` on **Stage 62** (the questioned 16.5h stage) → captured stack trace
   - Click `+details` on **Stage 71** (currently active stage) → captured stack trace
   - Click `+details` on **Stage 36** (KNOWN-GOOD MT #1 write, verified 2026-05-19 via gsutil `_SUCCESS` listing) → captured stack trace as reference
   
   All three stack traces are **structurally identical** modulo JIT class IDs:
   ```
   org.apache.spark.SparkContext.runJob(SparkContext.scala:2446)
   is.hail.backend.spark.SparkBackend.parallelizeAndComputeWithIndex(SparkBackend.scala:449)
   is.hail.backend.BackendUtils.collectDArray(BackendUtils.scala:85)
   __C{N}Compile.__m{M}begin_group_1_region40_91(Emit.scala)
   __C{N}Compile.__m{M}begin_group_1(Emit.scala)
   __C{N}Compile.__m{N+2}split_Block(Emit.scala)
   __C{N}Compile.apply(Emit.scala)
   is.hail.expr.ir.CompileAndEvaluate$.$anonfun$_apply$5(CompileAndEvaluate.scala:83)
   ...
   ```
   
   Since Stage 36 is a **verified successful write**, this stack pattern IS what Hail's write looks like (the write/Parquet-encode/GCS-upload operations are JIT-compiled into the `__C*Compile.__m*` bytecode classes themselves, not exposed as separate stack frames). Therefore Stages 45 and 62 (identical stack) are also writes, and the cascade Stages 46-51 + 63-70 (canonical Hail 82-task-rows-write + per-component _SUCCESS markers) confirm successful commits. **MT #2 + MT #3 strongly inferred committed.**
   
   Additional corroborating evidence: 5 wide+82-task-cascade event pairs observed throughout the run (Stages 10, 19, 36, 45, 62) — three correspond to MT #1/#2/#3 writes; two (Stages 10, 19) likely correspond to source MT reads + naive_coalesce materialization stages in load_qc_cohort.

Output: `n samples=414830`, `call_rate mean=0.9388 stdev=0.0042`. Empirically proved aggregate_cols functional on this kernel/JVM — ruled out the orphan-state / websocket-drop hypothesis for the original 14.6h hang.

**Action 5 — Cell 3 AFR primary re-fire (fired ~21:14:30 UTC; halted ~24:00 UTC at T+~3h 30min).** Healthy signals throughout the monitoring window:

| Wall T+ | JVM 8799 CPU | Δ CPU | %CPU instant | hail.log mtime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 09:10 (baseline) | — | — | — |

RSS stable 7.84 GB throughout (well under -Xmx 85.7 GB). No errors. No `_temporary` write artifacts (expected — write phase is the last step). hail.log silence after 21:16:01 initially looked alarming but was diagnosed as expected Hail-driver-log-quiet-during-Spark-executor-stages pattern (matched smoke-test mid-phase signature).

**Halt-condition refinement during monitoring.** The strict initial rule "10+ min static hail.log → halt" was empirically falsified by the smoke test (which ran 43 of its 44 min with same py-spy stack and same log silence and SUCCEEDED). Refined criterion (adopted as the operative rule going forward): "JVM driver CPU does not advance ≥ 30s of CPU time in 10 min wall window → halt (true wedge)." This catches actual dead-JVM signature without false-positive on healthy mid-stage compute.

**Action 6 — Cluster diagnostic via YARN RM REST API (fired from scratch_bootstrap at T+3h 17min).** Critical finding:

- `totalNodes: 16` (matches expected worker count)
- `runningContainers: 32` (50% cluster utilization — all available executor slots occupied)
- `pendingContainers: 3779` ← Spark demands ~60× more parallelism than the cluster can serve
- `pendingMB: 21283328` (~20.8 TB of pending memory requests)
- YARN-reported `progress: 10.0%` at 6h 45min app elapsed (Cell 3 itself ~3h 30min in)

**Wave-scheduling math:** 3811 demand / 32 supply = ~119 task-wave cycles per stage → ~70h+ projection for Cell 3 AFR alone. Forecasted all-3-ancestries (Cell 3 + 4 + 5) cost: ~$1250-1500 over 250-300h on this cluster. Not viable.

Per [[feedback_aou_use_persistent_disk]] Rule 1-Dataproc: Delete the env, never Pause.

```python
import subprocess

script = r"""
set -e

cd ~/coloc_analysis 2>/dev/null \
  || cd ~/repos/coloc_analysis 2>/dev/null \
  || cd /home/jupyter/coloc_analysis 2>/dev/null \
  || { echo "ERROR: clone not found in standard locations"; exit 2; }
echo "--- clone path ---"
pwd

echo ""
echo "--- pre-pull branch + status ---"
echo "branch: $(git branch --show-current)"
echo "status (empty = clean):"
git status --short || true

echo ""
echo "--- pre-pull HEAD ---"
git log --oneline -3

echo ""
echo "--- git pull origin main ---"
git pull origin main

echo ""
echo "--- post-pull HEAD (expect c2f29df / e7063f5 / 5389a88 / 36e8062 visible) ---"
git log --oneline -5

echo ""
echo "--- template structure check ---"
python3 << 'PYEOF'
import json
n = json.load(open('.planning/notebooks/AOU-1_template.ipynb'))
print(f'total cells: {len(n["cells"])}')
for i, c in enumerate(n['cells']):
    src = c['source']
    head = (src[0] if isinstance(src, list) and src else str(src))[:75].replace('\n', ' ')
    print(f'  cell {i}: type={c["cell_type"]:8s} head={head!r}')
PYEOF

echo ""
echo "--- workspace-root AOU-1 location ---"
ls -la /home/jupyter/AOU-1_cohort_definition.ipynb 2>/dev/null \
  || find /home/jupyter -maxdepth 3 -name 'AOU-1_cohort_definition.ipynb' 2>/dev/null \
  || echo "(workspace-root file not found in /home/jupyter; will write fresh)"
"""

result = subprocess.run(['bash'], input=script, capture_output=True, text=True)
print(result.stdout)
if result.stderr:
    print("--- stderr ---")
    print(result.stderr)
print(f"--- exit code: {result.returncode} ---")
```

### Finding 2026-04-14 PM — Phase 0 idempotency gap

Dry-run inspection of Phase A narrow-scout targets revealed:

- `download_ldsc_baseline` rule (src/snakemake/rules/pathway.smk:180-237) re-fetches 4 large tarballs (~5 GB total) from `broad-alkesgroup-ukbb-ld.s3.amazonaws.com` + GCS `requester-pays` paths. Manually-staged data IS on disk (`data/reference/ldsc/baselineLD.{1..22}.{annot.gz,l2.M,l2.M_5_50}`, `data/reference/ldsc/eur_w_ld_chr/w_hm3.snplist`, `data/reference/ldsc/w_hm3.snplist`) but the rule's flag file `data/reference/ldsc/.baseline_download_done` doesn't exist, so Snakemake re-runs.
- `download_magma_binary` rule expects `tools/magma_v1.10/magma` which doesn't exist; Carter's manual download landed at `data/reference/magma/magma`. Path mismatch + JS-gated CNCR upstream.
- `harmonize_sumstats` chains `download_sumstats` for 8 trait/ancestry combos (bmi.EUR, t2d.EUR, t2d.AFR, hypertension.EUR, asthma.EUR, asthma.AFR, stroke.EUR, stroke.AFR). Raw sumstats not on disk. Multiple URL-rot risk.
- Existing harmonized .bgz files (Feb 11 timestamps, ~2.3 GB at `data/processed/region_analysis/sumstats_harmonized_fixed/`) would be overwritten because `config/datasets.yaml` mtime is newer.

**Decision:** No real-data execution until Phase 0 download rules are made idempotent (skip-if-on-disk semantics) AND the magma binary path is reconciled. Routed to `/gsd-quick`.
